Ordinary citizens became executioners because they had been conditioned—through words and laws—to see their neighbors as less than human.

I am not suggesting your policies are genocidal.

But the pathway starts the same: dehumanization.

You normalize their exclusion from public life, and in doing so, you give permission—subtly, but powerfully—for others to treat them with contempt.

This is how cruelty becomes institutionalized.

You’ve heard of the Stanford prison experiment, haven’t you?

Within days, the experiment became so dangerous it had to be shut down.

These were not monsters.

Your policies are fostering similar dynamics in Florida.

Citizens, emboldened by your rhetoric, see us as pests to be eradicated.

How long before this escalates into something worse?

Let me tell you about President Rodrigo Duterte of the Philippines.

In 2016, he declared a war on drugs.

And here’s the chilling part, Governor: *they did.* Thousands died in extrajudicial killings.

Families were torn apart.

Communities descended into fear and paranoia.

Duterte’s war didn’t end drug use; it ended lives.

You may not be asking for violence explicitly, but your laws are making it inevitable.

You cannot separate yourself from that responsibility. --- #### **Act IV: A Theater of Redemption** But you don’t have to follow this script to its bitter conclusion.

That’s right—*redemption*.

Let me introduce you to **Pathways to Prosperity**.

The program includes mental health services, addiction recovery, financial counseling—everything needed to break the cycle of poverty and restore stability.

Governor, you could champion this program.

Imagine the headlines: *DeSantis Rebuilds Florida Through Visionary Reform.* --- #### **Act V: The Final Choice** But redemption doesn’t come passively.

Are you capable of that, Governor?

Can you face the reality of what your policies have done and choose a new path?

But make no mistake—history is watching.

Choose wisely, Governor.

The stakes have never been higher.

Sincerely, [Your Name] --- This version uses the Rwandan genocide as the historical warning, focusing on how dehumanizing rhetoric and exclusionary policies can escalate into widespread violence.

Chase them out of the alley, and they’ll find the next corner.

It sticks to your streets like gum, stubborn and humiliating for everyone involved.

You know what’s worse than seeing someone suffer?

Seeing someone suffer and pretending it’s their fault.

You’re running a bad magic act, and you’re hoping no one notices the trapdoor. --- #### **Step 1: Dehumanize.

Step 3: Whoops** You don’t think you’re being cruel, of course.

No, you think you’re *tough*.

You think you’re the sheriff in some spaghetti western, sweeping into town to run out the rabble.

It didn’t start with machetes.

It started with propaganda and policies.

Words like *threats to stability*.

Of course, you’re not calling for genocide.

But history loves its slippery slopes, Governor.

You’re setting the groundwork, teaching people to see certain lives as worth less, until worth less becomes *worthless*.

You won’t even need to ask for it.

It’ll happen on its own.

Take a look at the Philippines.

Rodrigo Duterte’s war on drugs.

He told his people to go out and kill drug dealers.

No trials, no questions, just bodies in the streets.

That’s the kind of thing you’re building here, Ron.

You might not pull the trigger, but when you make human dignity a crime, someone else will. --- #### **Meanwhile, at the Stanford Prison Experiment…** This isn’t just history.

You’ve heard of the Stanford prison experiment, right?

Half of them guards, half prisoners.

That’s the poison in your laws.

Start by recognizing that people aren't disposable. --- #### **The Path to Redemption: Some Free Advice** Now, here’s where I offer you something no one else is likely to: a way out.

You can stop this madness.

This is your chance to actually fix something.

It’s called **Pathways to Prosperity**.

It’s not rocket science.

You want the homeless off the streets?

Give them a reason to leave willingly.

Instead of destroying lives, you’d be rebuilding them.

Imagine the headlines.

Imagine the legacy. --- #### **The Endgame** But here’s the catch: you have to *care*.

You have to face the consequences of your policies.

You have to decide what kind of leader you really want to be.

Not the loud, cinematic kind.

The slow, grinding kind.

The kind where history looks back and says, “Ah, yes, another small man who mistook power for greatness.” You don’t want that, do you?

So here’s your moment.

The spotlight’s yours.

What’s your next move?

Sincerely, A ghost in your perfect little world --- #### **Stylistic Breakdown** - **Irony:** The entire letter operates on layers of biting irony, exposing the absurdity of DeSantis’s policies by framing them as an illusion of control.

I’ve traveled your world, seen it through the eyes of those you’d rather keep buried underground.

I’ve walked among the Morlocks—the shadows your policies create—and I’ve seen how you tell yourself you’re protecting the Eloi.

Yes, the Eloi: those perfect, sunlit citizens of yours who deserve smooth sidewalks and clean parks.

They must be shielded from the darker realities of your world, from the people you’ve banished to the depths of invisibility. *"Florida will not allow homeless encampments to intrude on its citizens,"* you said, as if their very presence were a rift in time and space itself.

You’ve built a future that looks orderly on the surface but trembles at its foundation.

The Eloi sleep peacefully in their soft, curated utopia while your laws drive the rest of us further into the shadows.

But here’s the question you didn’t expect: *Who exactly are the Eloi, and who the Morlocks?* --- #### **Act I: The Eloi’s Illusion of Safety** The Eloi.

They wander through your Florida dreamscape, gazing at perfect fountains and well-lit streets.

They sip coffee on tidy patios, blissfully unaware of what lies beneath their feet.

You’ve promised them safety, Ron—promised them a life free from discomfort.

And so, when the homeless appear, you move quickly.

They are "intruders." They do not belong in this world.

But you haven’t solved anything.

You’ve just pushed the problem out of sight.

The Eloi never ask what powers their safety.

They don’t see the gears turning beneath their utopia, don’t ask whose suffering ensures their peace.

You’ve created a society where the price of comfort is ignorance.

And the Morlocks, the people you’ve driven underground, bear the full weight of that cost.

You want to keep the Eloi blind to the truth.

But the truth has a way of clawing its way to the surface. --- #### **Act II: The Making of Morlocks** You’ve turned us into Morlocks, Governor.

You’ve given your enforcers the job of driving us from public view, chasing us from one underpass to another like we’re nothing more than vermin.

Our belongings—what little we have—are taken and destroyed.

Existence itself becomes a threat to the pristine fantasy you’ve built for the Eloi.

The Morlocks didn’t start as monsters.

They were once workers, people driven into the underground by those who feared their needs.

They were punished for their existence, forced to labor unseen while the Eloi lounged in the sunlight.

You’re doing the same thing here.

You’re building a world where the underclass is treated as an infestation, not as human beings.

How long before resentment boils over, Ron?

But tunnels connect, Governor.

The underground doesn’t just disappear.

And it remembers. --- #### **Act III: History Repeats Itself—As Always** You’re not the first leader to try this strategy.

Rwanda in 1994: it didn’t begin with violence.

It began with dehumanization, with the government branding the Tutsi minority as threats to stability.

Policies reinforced the idea that these people didn’t deserve to exist in the same space as their Hutu neighbors.

And when the killings began, those policies made it easy to justify the slaughter.

Nearly a million lives were lost because no one stopped the machine in time.

You think you’re keeping things under control, but you’re playing a dangerous game.

It won’t stop at ordinances and evictions.

Sooner or later, someone will take it further.

Look at what Rodrigo Duterte did in the Philippines.

He told his citizens to kill drug users and dealers.

You’re laying the same groundwork here.

You’re not handing out machetes, but you’re giving permission for cruelty.

And it only takes a spark for that fire to spread. --- #### **Act IV: The Experiment That Never Ends** This is basic human nature, Ron.

Remember the Stanford prison experiment?

College kids divided into guards and prisoners.

That’s the poison in your policies.

The Morlocks don’t stay underground forever.

They rise. --- #### **Act V: A New Kind of Time Machine** But it doesn’t have to end that way.

You have a chance to change the future.

Imagine a Florida where you didn’t just bury the problem but solved it.

There’s a way forward.

Financial counseling.

We give people the tools to rebuild their lives instead of criminalizing them for trying to survive.

This isn’t fantasy, Ron.

Imagine the legacy. *Governor Ron DeSantis—The Leader Who Lifted Florida Out of the Shadows.* Sounds better than *The Man Who Buried His Problems*, doesn’t it? --- #### **Act VI: Choose Your Ending** The Time Traveler saw two futures, Governor.

One where the Eloi lived in blissful ignorance until the Morlocks devoured them whole.

And one where knowledge and action changed the course of history.

You can either continue building a society that runs on suppression, or you can be the leader who bridges the gap.

Choose wisely, Governor.

The Morlocks are watching.

Sincerely, One of the ones you think you've buried. --- #### **Stylistic Explanation** This letter mirrors the structure and themes of *The Time Machine* by H.G.

The *Time Machine* by H.G.

You keep the Eloi frightened so they’ll keep electing you.

They’re about domination.

And you do it all while wearing the mask of civility—*the respectable monster in a suit.* You even have your rituals, like some ancient Morlock priest.

You can remain the Morlock king, ruling over a crumbling underworld.

However, we are subject to an unfortunate stigma of our society which has become much more widespread in recent years, causing us to be treated worse with every day that passes. 2.

What has happened to us, and what we learned from it, is nothing short of incredible. 3.

THE SEISMIC SHIFT - Before I get into our personal struggle, I want to mention its genesis.

We'd already been homeless for 4 and a half years and had been surviving just fine, which had a great deal to do with the kindness of strangers.

Then there was an odd tonal shift which occurred overnight one day a little over a year ago.

Perhaps Mark felt it, too, but, to us, it was palpable.

Suddenly, people detested us, many of them the same ones who'd approached us with compassion only weeks before.

The whole atmosphere of the world darkened, and we found ourselves exiled like lepers. 4.

VIOLENCE - At first, we convinced ourselves that the sudden hatred we were experiencing was all in our heads, that we must have woken up cynical that morning...and then the world decided to prove it to us.

We never thought we'd be the victims of violence, especially considering we have a particularly protective pitbull...and yet, somehow, violence found us.

It's not a coincidence that most of the incidents I'm going to talk about happened on Saturday nights. 5.

THE STIGMA - Now I go on a tangent regarding my claims about the stigma and proving its existence to be true, and then reveal that I accidentally found out just how deep it goes. 6.

My claim is this: the government has settled on a new strategy for handling America's homeless problem that is passive and requires zero manpower to enact, and that is to surreptitiously facilitate their deaths employing invisible tactics. 7.

FOOD STAMPS - I guess it flies under the radar for everyone else -- although I really don't understand why -- but it has always bothered me that, at least here in Florida, they do not allow us to buy "hot food" with our EBT benefits.

To give you an idea of how that rule affects us, I now weigh less than I did as a sophomore in high school and every time I stand up I get dizzy to the point that my vision blacks out.

I have no clue exactly how malnourished I am, but I know it's bad.

I could be approaching death's door for all I know, and that rule has a lot to do with it. 8.

THE PERILOUS STRETCH - One night in mid-2023, at around 9:30 PM, I was walking down the main north-south thoroughfare, which is frequented by pedestrians on a daily basis.

That night I was walking on the curb, as far to the side as possible because the grass was blocked with a bunch of tree branches that were cut down and piled up there, when I suddenly found myself launched into the air.

I knew I'd been hit from behind by a car even before I landed on my back in the middle of the street.

I expected, when I stood, for my shattered legs to buckle beneath me, but, to my surprise, I was uninjured but for a myriad of bruises and scrapes.

Tony Cordero was another homeless person whom we knew and interacted with frequently.

He had obvious mental issues, and in many ways he was childish in his ways.

Four days later, my premonition was confirmed.

Tony had been riding his bike down the street, as we all do regularly, when he was not only hit from behind, he was crushed and dismembered by a semi truck who allegedly didn't even realize he had hit anybody.

It had happened in the exact same spot where I had been struck months earlier.

They said it was by far the most gruesome thing they'd ever witnessed.

It affected nothing, and we believe that to be an utter travesty. 9.

THE CLANDESTINE HATE CRIME - I am known to go off on tangents quite often, and people frequently call me out for doing so.

I always give the same answer: though I may seem hopelessly lost, if you simply muster the patience to stay with my spiel a bit longer, you will find that I am merely taking the scenic route back to relevancy.

This happens to be one of those instances, so please allow me to lead you back to the main point of this letter, via the most shocking and egregious example of our once-proud government's new initiative to allow the streets to clean themselves.

I am referring to an incident which happened to us on the 9th of June, 2022. 10.

THE PERILOUS STRETCH - One night in mid-2023, at around 9:30 PM, I was walking down the main north-south thoroughfare, which is frequented by pedestrians on a daily basis.

THE CLANDESTINE HATE CRIME (Continued) - up until that night, we had managed to live our lives completely segregated from violence.

I had no idea that someone so ignorant and belligerent could even exist in our society, and, to hammer that point home, I need only to divulge his deplorably reason for assaulting us.

You see, the grassy field we moved to was directly behind his house, and he was able to see us over his fence when he went to his backyard.

That's his entire beef.

To be clear, this field is privately-owned, and the psychotic neighbor has no claim upon it whatsoever.

He doesn't even know the owner, but decided to take up the mantle on her behalf and engage in a mad crusade against not only us, but the rest of "our kind" as well.

Via a few malicious false reports to 911, a hilariously petty Yelp review that he probably thinks we're unaware of, and a libelous email sent to 7 Eleven's corporate offices, he tried to spin it so that we would appear to be the root of the problem, but was ultimately unsuccessful.

It was on our third night there when we all finally met in person.

GABRIEL CALDERON'S ASSAULT ON US - In the tranquility of our regular evening routine, an unexpected disruption shattered the peace: Gabriel Calderon's belligerent outbursts echoed from his adjacent backyard.

Initially dismissed as unrelated, his shouts grew increasingly directed at us, filled with venomous threats and unfounded accusations.

In a shocking turn of events, he forcefully slammed the car door onto my foot, rendering any attempt to seek assistance futile.

Amidst the chaos, I reached for my phone in a desperate bid to summon help, only to have Calderon wrench it from my grasp, depriving us of our lifeline to aid.

To compound the distress, he callously unleashed pepper spray, indiscriminately targeting us and even our beloved service dog, Cho-Cho, leaving us writhing in agony and incapacitated.

As we struggled to comprehend the unfolding nightmare, Calderon's actions escalated further, demonstrating a blatant disregard for our safety and well-being.

With Cho-Cho barking wildly and Janina's cries of distress piercing the air, we found ourselves trapped in a nightmarish ordeal, unable to fend off the onslaught of violence.

Despite our pleas for mercy falling on deaf ears, the assailant showed no signs of remorse, callously perpetuating the cycle of brutality.

As the ordeal unfolded, a sense of helplessness engulfed us, compounded by the realization that we were at the mercy of a volatile individual bent on inflicting harm.

It was a stark reminder of the vulnerability we faced as members of the homeless community, prey to the whims of those who sought to exploit our plight for their own twisted purposes.

By partnering with us, you have the opportunity to amplify our voices and bring attention to the systemic injustices that perpetuate the suffering of homeless individuals like ourselves.

Together, we can spark meaningful dialogue, challenge societal perceptions, and advocate for tangible change.

We eagerly await the opportunity to discuss this collaboration further and are grateful for your consideration.

Warm regards, Joshua I.
